5 There was in those days of Herod, that king of Judea, a certain priest, whose name was Zacharias, of that course of Abijah, and his wife was of the daughters of Aaron, and her name was Elizabeth. 6 And both were righteous before the Lord, as they walked in all the commandments and justifications of God; they were blameless. 7 And they had no children, because Elizabeth was barren and both had advanced in their days. 8 And it happened, when he was performing the priesthood in that ordered ministry of his days before God, 9 According to the priesthood, it fell to him to place incense when he had entered that temple of the Lord. 10 And all that multitude was praying outside at that hour of incense. 11 An Angel of the Lord manifested himself to him, standing at the right of that altar of incense.

12 And Zacharias was troubled when he had seen, and fear fell upon him. 13 That Angel of the Lord said to him: "Do not fear, Zacharias, because your prayers have been made audible; and your wife Elizabeth will bear you a son, and you shall call his name John. 14 And there will be joy and gladness for you, and many will rejoice at that birth of his. 15 For he will be great before the Lord; and he will not drink wine and strong drink and intoxicating liquor, and he will be full of the Holy Spirit even from his mother's womb. 16 And he will turn many of those sons of Israel to the Lord their God. 17 And he himself will go before him in the spirit and power of Elijah, to turn the hearts of the fathers to the sons, the disobedient to the prudence of the righteous, to prepare for the Lord an instructed people." 18 And Zacharias said to that

22 Blessed will you be, when men shall hate you, and when they shall exclude you and revile you, and cast out your name as evil, for the sake of the Son of man. 23 Rejoice and be glad; for behold, your reward is great in heaven; for in the same way their fathers used to do to the prophets. 24 But woe to you, rich, for you have received your consolation. 25 Woe to you who are full now, for you shall hunger. Woe to you who laugh now, for you shall mourn and weep. 26 Woe to you, when all men speak well of you; for in the same way their fathers used to do to the false prophets. 27 But I say to you who hear: Love your enemies, do good to those who hate you. 28 And bless those who curse you, and pray for those who mistreat you. 29 To him who strikes you on the cheek, offer the other also. And from him who takes away your garment, do not withhold your tunic either. 30 Give to everyone who asks you; and from him who takes away what is yours, do not demand it back. 31 And just as you want men to do to you, you also do to them likewise.

And when he was not far from the house, the centurion sent friends, and said to him: "Lord, do not trouble yourself; for I am not worthy that you should enter under the roof of my house. 7 Therefore I did not even think myself worthy to come to you; but speak the word, and my servant will be healed. 8 For I also am a man placed under authority, and I have soldiers under me; and I say to one, 'Go,' and he goes; and to another, 'Come,' and he comes; and to my servant, 'Do this,' and he does it." 9 When Jesus heard this, he marveled at his faith, and turned to the crowd that followed him and said: "Amen I say to you, not even in Israel have I found such great faith." 10 And those who had been sent returned to the house and found the servant alive.

11 And it came to pass after this, that he went into a city, which they call Nain, and his disciples and a great crowd were going with him. 12 And he drew near to the gates of the city; and behold, a dead man was being carried out, the only begotten son of his mother; and she was a widow; and a great crowd of the city was with her. 13 And when Jesus saw her, he had compassion on her, and said to her: "Do not weep." 14 And he came to the bier, and touched it. And those who were carrying the bier stood still. And he said: "Young man, I say to you, arise." 15 And the dead man sat up, and began to speak. And they set sail. 23 And as they were sailing, he fell asleep, and a storm of wind descended upon the sea, and the boat was filling, and they were in danger. 24 And they came to him, and woke him, saying: "Master, we are perishing." And he rose, and rebuked the wind and the raging of the water, and they ceased; and there was a great calm. 25 And he said to them: "Where is your faith?" And they were afraid and marveled, saying to one another: "Who is this, that he commands even the winds and the water, and they obey him?" 26 And they sailed to the region of the Gerasenes, which is across from Galilee. 27 And when they had stepped out to the land, a certain man from the city met him, who had demons; and for a long time he had worn no clothes, and did not live in a house, but in the tombs. 28 And when he saw Jesus, he cried out, and fell down at his feet, and with a loud voice said: "What have I to do with you,

And he said to him: "Legion"; because many demons had entered into him. 31 And they begged him that he would not command them to go out into the abyss. 32 And there was a large herd of swine feeding there on the mountain, and they begged him that he would permit them to enter into those swine. And he permitted them. 33 And the demons went out from the man, and entered into the swine; and the whole herd rushed down the steep bank into the sea, and were drowned. 34 When those who fed them saw what had happened, they fled, and went and told it in the city and in the villages. 35 And they came to see what had happened, and came to Jesus; and they found the man from whom the demons had gone out, sitting, clothed, and in his right

and that of the Father, and of the holy Angels. 27 I tell you truly: There are some standing here, who shall not taste death, until they see the kingdom of God." 28 And it came to pass about eight days after these words, that Jesus took Peter, James, and John, and went up into the mountain to pray. 29 And as he prayed, the appearance of his face was altered, and his garment became white and dazzling. 30 And behold, two men were talking with him, who were Moses and Elijah, 31 Who appeared in glory, and spoke of his departure, which he was to accomplish at Jerusalem. 32 Now Peter and those who were with him were heavy with sleep. But when they were fully awake, they saw his glory, and the two men standing with him. 33 And it came to pass, as they were departing from him, Peter said to Jesus: "Master, it is good for us to be here; let us make three tabernacles here, one for you, and one for Moses, and one for Elijah"; for he did not know what he was saying. 34 And as he was saying this, a cloud came and overshadowed them; and they were afraid as they entered into the cloud. 35 And a voice came out of the cloud: "This is my beloved Son, hear him."

And they kept silent, and told no one in those days anything of what they had seen. 37 And it came to pass on the next day, when they had come down from the mountain, a great crowd met him. 38 And behold, a man from the crowd cried out, and said: "Master, I beg you, look upon my son, for he is my only child. 39 And an evil spirit seizes him, and he suddenly cries out; and it convulses him and he foams; and it hardly departs from him, and bruises him. 40 And I begged your disciples to cast it out, and they could not." 41 Jesus answered and said: "O faithless and perverse generation, how long shall I be with you, and bear with you? Bring your son here." 42 And as he was still coming, the demon threw him down and convulsed him. Jesus rebuked the unclean spirit, and healed the boy and gave him back to his father. 43 And they were all amazed at the greatness of God; and all were marveling at all that Jesus was doing. X. 1 After this the Lord appointed seventy-two others, and sent them two by two before his face into every city and village, wherever he himself was about to enter. 2 And he said to them, "The harvest is plentiful, but the laborers are few. Your kingdom come. Your will be done. 3 Give us our daily bread of substance. 4 And forgive us our sins; for we also forgive everyone who is indebted to us. 39 The Lord said to him, "Now you Pharisees make clean the outside of the cup and the dish; but your inside is full of rapine and wickedness. 40 Fools, did not he who made the outside also make the inside? 41 Nevertheless, give alms of what you have, and behold, all things are clean to you. 42 But woe to you, Pharisees, for you tithe mint and rue and all manner of herbs, and pass by judgment and the love of God. XII. 1 In which things, when a very great multitude had gathered, so that they even trampled upon one another, he began to say first to his disciples, "Beware of the leaven of the Pharisees, which is hypocrisy. 2 But there is nothing hidden, that will not be revealed, and secret, that will not be known. 3 Therefore, whatever you say in darkness will be heard in the light; and what you speak in the ear in the inner rooms will be proclaimed upon the housetops. 4 But I say to you, my friends: Do not be afraid of those who kill the body and after this have nothing more that they can do to you. 5 But I will show you whom you should fear; fear him, who after he has killed has power to cast into hell; yes, I say to you, fear him. 6 Are not five sparrows sold for two farthings, and not one of them is hidden before God? 7 But even the hairs of your head are all numbered. Do not fear therefore: you are of more value than many sparrows.

8 Also I say to you: Everyone who confesses me before men, the Son of man also will confess him before the Angels of God. 9 And he who denies me before men, I will deny him also before the Angels of God. 10 And everyone who speaks a word against the Son of man, it will be forgiven him; but to him who blasphemes against the Holy Spirit it will not be forgiven. 11 And when they bring you before the synagogues and rulers and powers, do not be anxious about how or what word you should answer them or what you should say. 12 For the Holy Spirit will teach you in that hour what you ought to say." 13 One from that people said to him, "Master, tell my brother to divide the inheritance with me." 14 But he said to him, "Man, who made me a judge and a divider over you?" 15 And he said to them, "Take heed and beware of all covetousness, for one's life does not consist in the abundance of his possessions." 16 And he told them a parable also, and said to them, "The ground of a certain rich man brought forth plentifully. 17 And he thought within his heart, and said: What shall I do, because I have nowhere to gather this fruit of mine?

why are you anxious about the rest? 27 Consider the lilies how they grow; they neither labor, nor spin; but I say to you, that not even Solomon in all his glory was arrayed like one of these. 28 If God so clothes the grass in the field, which today is and tomorrow will be cast into the oven, how much more will he clothe you, O you of little faith? 29 And do not seek what you shall eat, and what you shall drink; and do not be of doubtful mind. 30 For all these things the nations of the world seek. No one is good, except God alone. 20 You know the commands: Do not commit adultery, do not kill a man, do not steal, do not bear false witness, give honor to your father and to your mother." 21 He however said to him: "All this I have kept from my youth."

22 When Jesus had heard this, he said to him: "One thing still this is lacking to you: all that you have, sell, and give to the poor, and you will have treasure in heaven; and come, and follow me." 23 When however he had heard this, he was saddened: for he was very rich. 24 When Jesus had seen him, he said: "Since it is difficult for him who will have riches to enter into the kingdom of God. 25 It is easier for a rope of a machine to pass "through" the eye of a needle, than for a rich man to enter into the kingdom of God." 26 They however who heard, said to him: "And who can be made safe?" 27 Jesus answered them, and said to them: "What is impossible from men is possible through God." 28 Peter said to him: "Behold we have left all our own, and have followed you." 29 He said to them: "Amen I say to you, because there is no one who will leave house, or parents or brothers, or wife, or children for the sake of the kingdom of God, 30 "Who will not" receive a manifold "part" in this time, and in that future age eternal life."

31 And he took aside those twelve, and said to them: "Behold I am going up to Jerusalem, and all the writings of the prophets will be completed upon the Son of man. 32 For he will be delivered into the hands of the gentiles, and they will mock him, and he will be affected with ignominy, and they will spit upon him. 33 And they will torture him and will kill him, and on the third day he will rise." 34 And they did not understand this; but that word was hidden from them, and they did not know that which was said. 35 And it happened, when they had approached to Jericho, a certain blind man was sitting upon the way, begging. 36 When he had heard that the multitude was passing by, he was asking them: "What is this?" 37 And they told him, that Jesus the Nazarene was passing by. 38 He however cried out, and said: "Jesus, son of David, have mercy on me." 39 And they who were going before were angry at him that he might be silent. He however was crying out more, and was saying: "Jesus, son of David, have mercy on me." 40 Jesus stood, and commanded him to be called. And, when he had approached he asked him: 41 "What do you want? And I will do for you." He however said to him: "Lord, that you may restore to me my eyes." 42 Jesus however said to him: "Receive them: your faith has made you safe."

33 Heaven and earth will pass away; but my words will not pass away. 34 Take heed to yourselves, lest perhaps your hearts be weighed down in indulgence and drunkenness and the anxiety of this world, and that day come upon you suddenly. 35 For as a snare it will come upon those sitting upon the face of all the earth. 36 Watch therefore at all times, and pray, that you may be deemed worthy to escape from all this which is to happen and to stand before the Son of man." 37 And he was staying in that temple by day, and was teaching; but by night he would go out and stay on that mountain, which they call "the name" of Olives. 38 And all the people would come in the morning to him in that temple, to hear his words.

6 And he confessed to them; and he was seeking an opportune time, so that he might deliver him to them without the people. 7 Those days of Unleavened Bread arrived, when it was fitting for that passover to be sacrificed. 8 And he sent Peter and John, and said to them: "Go, and prepare for us the passover, that we may eat." 9 But they said to him: "Where do you want? And we will prepare for you." 10 But he said to them: Lo, behold, at the very time you enter into the city, a man will meet you, who will be carrying a pitcher with water; follow him into the house into which he also will enter. 11 And say to the master of that house: The Teacher says to you: Where is the guest room, in which I may eat this passover with my disciples? 12 And he will show you a large furnished upper room; prepare for us there. 13 And they went, and found it as he had said to them, and they prepared that passover. 14 And when the hour had come, he sat down, and the twelve disciples with him. 15 And he said to them: "With desire I have desired to eat this passover with you, before I suffer. 16 But I tell you, I will no longer eat of it, until it is fulfilled in the kingdom of God." 17 And when he had taken the cup he gave thanks, and said: "Take this, and divide it among yourselves.

of Moses, and the Prophets, and the Psalms concerning me to be fulfilled." 45 Then he opened their minds, so that they might understand the Books. 46 And he said to them: "That thus it was necessary for the Christ to suffer, and to rise from the dead on the third day, 47 and for repentance and remission of sins to be preached in his name to all nations, beginning from Jerusalem. 48 And you are witnesses to all this. 49 And behold, I send the promise of my Father upon you; and you sit in this city of Jerusalem, until you are clothed with power from on high." 50 He led them out as far as Bethany; and he lifted up his hands, and blessed them. 51 And it happened, at the very time that he was blessing them, he departed from them. 52 And when they had worshipped him, they returned to Jerusalem with great joy. 53 And they were always in that temple, and were blessing God.
